1→"""
     2→Queue lifecycle management extracted from HeadlessTaskQueue.
     3→
     4→Contains start, stop, and submit_task logic.  Every public function takes the
     5→``HeadlessTaskQueue`` instance (aliased *queue*) as its first argument.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→import threading
    11→import time
    12→import traceback
    13→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING
    14→
    15→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    16→    from headless_model_management import HeadlessTaskQueue, GenerationTask
    17→
    18→from source.task_handlers.queue.task_processor import worker_loop, _monitor_loop
    19→
    20→
    21→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    22→# Start
    23→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    24→
    25→def start_queue(queue: "HeadlessTaskQueue", preload_model=None):
    26→    """
    27→    Start the task queue processing service.
    28→
    29→    Args:
    30→        queue: The HeadlessTaskQueue instance
    31→        preload_model: Optional model to pre-load before processing tasks.
    32→                      If specified, the model will be loaded immediately after
    33→                      workers start, making the first task much faster.
    34→                      Example: "wan_2_2_vace_lightning_baseline_2_2_2"
    35→    """
    36→    if queue.running:
    37→        queue.logger.warning("Queue already running")
    38→        return
    39→
    40→    queue.running = True
    41→    queue.shutdown_event.clear()
    42→
    43→    # Start worker threads
    44→    for i in range(queue.max_workers):
    45→        worker = threading.Thread(
    46→            target=worker_loop,
    47→            args=(queue,),
    48→            name=f"GenerationWorker-{i}",
    49→            daemon=True
    50→        )
    51→        worker.start()
    52→        queue.worker_threads.append(worker)
    53→
    54→    # Start monitoring thread
    55→    monitor = threading.Thread(
    56→        target=_monitor_loop,
    57→        args=(queue,),
    58→        name="QueueMonitor",
    59→        daemon=True
    60→    )
    61→    monitor.start()
    62→    queue.worker_threads.append(monitor)
    63→
    64→    queue.logger.info(f"Task queue started with {queue.max_workers} workers")
    65→
    66→    # Pre-load model if specified
    67→    if preload_model:
    68→        queue.logger.info(f"Pre-loading model: {preload_model}")
    69→        try:
    70→            # Initialize orchestrator and load model in background
    71→            queue._ensure_orchestrator()
    72→            if queue.orchestrator is None:
    73→                raise RuntimeError("Orchestrator initialization failed during preload")
    74→            queue.orchestrator.load_model(preload_model)
    75→            queue.current_model = preload_model
    76→            queue.logger.info(f"Model {preload_model} pre-loaded successfully")
    77→        except (RuntimeError, ValueError, OSError) as e:
    78→            # Log the full error with traceback
    79→            queue.logger.error(f"FATAL: Failed to pre-load model {preload_model}: {e}\n{traceback.format_exc()}")
    80→
    81→            # If orchestrator failed to initialize, this is fatal - worker cannot function
    82→            if queue.orchestrator is None:
    83→                queue.logger.error("Orchestrator failed to initialize - worker cannot process tasks. Exiting.")
    84→                raise RuntimeError(f"Orchestrator initialization failed during preload: {e}") from e
    85→            else:
    86→                # Orchestrator is OK but model load failed - this is recoverable
    87→                queue.logger.warning(f"Model {preload_model} failed to load, but orchestrator is ready. Worker will continue.")
    88→
    89→
    90→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    91→# Stop
    92→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    93→
    94→def stop_queue(queue: "HeadlessTaskQueue", timeout: float = 30.0):
    95→    """Stop the task queue processing service."""
    96→    if not queue.running:
    97→        return
    98→
    99→    queue.logger.info("Shutting down task queue...")
   100→    queue.running = False
```

1→"""Generation strategy convenience methods for WanOrchestrator.
     2→
     3→Provides typed wrappers (T2V, VACE, Flux, config-based) around the core
     4→:meth:`WanOrchestrator.generate` method.  Each function is bound to the
     5→orchestrator at class-definition time via ``orchestrator.py``.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Optional
     9→
    10→from source.core.log import generation_logger
    11→
    12→if TYPE_CHECKING:
    13→    from source.core.params import TaskConfig
    14→

    16→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    17→# Text-to-Video
    18→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    19→
    20→def generate_t2v(self, prompt: str, model_type: str = None, **kwargs) -> str:
    21→    """Generate text-to-video content.
    22→
    23→    Thin wrapper around :meth:`WanOrchestrator.generate` that adds a
    24→    warning if the currently-loaded model is not a T2V model.
    25→
    26→    Args:
    27→        self: WanOrchestrator instance.
    28→        prompt: Text prompt for generation.
    29→        model_type: Optional model override.
    30→        **kwargs: Additional parameters forwarded to generate().
    31→
    32→    Returns:
    33→        Path to generated video file.
    34→    """
    35→    if not self._is_t2v():
    36→        generation_logger.warning(f"Current model {self.current_model} may not be optimized for T2V")
    37→    return self.generate(prompt=prompt, model_type=model_type, **kwargs)
    38→
    39→
    40→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    41→# VACE (Video Any Controllable Edit)
    42→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    43→
    44→def generate_vace(
    45→    self,
    46→    prompt: str,
    47→    video_guide: str,
    48→    model_type: str = None,
    49→    video_mask: Optional[str] = None,
    50→    video_prompt_type: str = "VP",
    51→    control_net_weight: float = 1.0,
    52→    control_net_weight2: float = 1.0,
    53→    **kwargs,
    54→) -> str:
    55→    """Generate VACE controlled video content.
    56→
    57→    Thin wrapper around :meth:`WanOrchestrator.generate` that adds
    58→    a warning if the currently-loaded model is not a VACE model.
    59→
    60→    Args:
    61→        self: WanOrchestrator instance.
    62→        prompt: Text prompt for generation.
    63→        video_guide: Path to primary control video (required).
    64→        model_type: Optional model override.
    65→        video_mask: Path to primary mask video (optional).
    66→        video_prompt_type: VACE encoding type (e.g., "VP", "VPD", "VPDA").
    67→        control_net_weight: Strength for first VACE encoding.
    68→        control_net_weight2: Strength for second VACE encoding.
    69→        **kwargs: Additional parameters forwarded to generate().
    70→
    71→    Returns:
    72→        Path to generated video file.
    73→    """
    74→    if not self._is_vace():
    75→        generation_logger.warning(f"Current model {self.current_model} may not be a VACE model")
    76→
    77→    # Log LoRA parameters at VACE level
    78→    generation_logger.debug(f"generate_vace received kwargs: {list(kwargs.keys())}")
    79→    if "lora_names" in kwargs:
    80→        generation_logger.debug(f"generate_vace lora_names: {kwargs['lora_names']}")
    81→    if "lora_multipliers" in kwargs:
    82→        generation_logger.debug(f"generate_vace lora_multipliers: {kwargs['lora_multipliers']}")
    83→
    84→    return self.generate(
    85→        prompt=prompt,
    86→        model_type=model_type,
    87→        video_guide=video_guide,
    88→        video_mask=video_mask,
    89→        video_prompt_type=video_prompt_type,
    90→        control_net_weight=control_net_weight,
    91→        control_net_weight2=control_net_weight2,
    92→        **kwargs,
    93→    )
    94→
    95→
    96→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    97→# Flux image generation
    98→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    99→
   100→def generate_flux(self, prompt: str, images: int = 4, model_type: str = None, **kwargs) -> str:
   101→    """Generate Flux images.
   102→
   103→    Thin wrapper around :meth:`WanOrchestrator.generate` that adds a
   104→    warning if the currently-loaded model is not a Flux model.
   105→
   106→    Args:
   107→        self: WanOrchestrator instance.
   108→        prompt: Text prompt for generation.
   109→        images: Number of images to generate (maps to video_length).
   110→        model_type: Optional model override.
   111→        **kwargs: Additional parameters forwarded to generate().
   112→
   113→    Returns:
   114→        Path to generated image(s).
   115→    """
   116→    if not self._is_flux():
   117→        generation_logger.warning(f"Current model {self.current_model} may not be a Flux model")
   118→
   119→    return self.generate(
   120→        prompt=prompt,
   121→        model_type=model_type,
   122→        video_length=images,  # For Flux, video_length = number of images
   123→        **kwargs,
   124→    )
   125→
   126→
   127→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   128→# Config-based generation (Qwen / universal typed API)
   129→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   130→
   131→def generate_with_config(self, config: 'TaskConfig') -> str:
   132→    """Generate content using a typed TaskConfig object.
   133→
   134→    This is the new typed API that provides cleaner parameter handling:
   135→    1. All parameters are already parsed and validated in TaskConfig
   136→    2. LoRAs are already resolved (downloaded if needed)
   137→    3. Phase config is already applied
   138→
   139→    Works for all model types (T2V, VACE, Flux, Qwen) but is the
   140→    primary entry-point for Qwen-style image generation.
   141→
   142→    Args:
   143→        self: WanOrchestrator instance.
   144→        config: TaskConfig object with all generation parameters.
   145→
   146→    Returns:
   147→        Path to generated output file(s).
   148→
   149→    Raises:
   150→        TypeError: If config is not a TaskConfig instance.
```

1→"""
     2→Join final stitch handler - stitch all clips and transitions together in one pass.
     3→
     4→This is the second phase of the parallel join architecture:
     5→1. Multiple join_clips_segment tasks generate transitions in parallel (transition_only=True)
     6→2. This task stitches all original clips + transitions together in a single encode pass
     7→"""
     8→
     9→import json
    10→from pathlib import Path
    11→from typing import Tuple
    12→
    13→# Import shared utilities
    14→from ...utils import (
    15→    get_video_frame_count_and_fps,
    16→    download_video_if_url,
    17→    prepare_output_path_with_upload,
    18→    upload_and_get_final_output_location)
    19→from ...media.video import (
    20→    extract_frames_from_video,
    21→    extract_frame_range_to_video,
    22→    stitch_videos_with_crossfade,
    23→    add_audio_to_video)
    24→from ... import db_operations as db_ops
    25→from source.core.log import orchestrator_logger
    26→
    27→def handle_join_final_stitch(
    28→    task_params_from_db: dict,
    29→    main_output_dir_base: Path,
    30→    task_id: str) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
    31→    """
    32→    Handle join_final_stitch task: stitch all clips and transitions together in one pass.
    33→
    34→    This is the second phase of the parallel join architecture:
    35→    1. Multiple join_clips_segment tasks generate transitions in parallel (transition_only=True)
    36→    2. This task stitches all original clips + transitions together in a single encode pass
    37→
    38→    Args:
    39→        task_params_from_db: Task parameters including:
    40→            - clip_list: List of original clip dicts with 'url' and optional 'name'
    41→            - transition_task_ids: List of transition task IDs to fetch outputs from
    42→            - gap_from_clip1: Frames to trim from end of each clip (except last)
    43→            - gap_from_clip2: Frames to trim from start of each clip (except first)
    44→            - blend_frames: Frames to crossfade at each boundary
    45→            - fps: Output FPS
    46→            - audio_url: Optional audio to add to final output
    47→        main_output_dir_base: Base output directory
    48→        task_id: Task ID for logging
    49→
    50→    Returns:
    51→        Tuple of (success: bool, output_path_or_message: str)
    52→    """
    53→    orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[FINAL_STITCH] Task {task_id}: Starting final stitch handler")
    54→
    55→    try:
    56→        # --- Chain Mode Passthrough ---
    57→        # When created by the chain pattern, the last join in the chain already produced
    58→        # the fully concatenated video. We just pass through its output (+ optional audio).
    59→        chain_mode = task_params_from_db.get("chain_mode", False)
    60→        if chain_mode:
    61→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[FINAL_STITCH] Task {task_id}: Chain mode \u2014 passthrough from last chain join")
    62→            transition_task_ids = task_params_from_db.get("transition_task_ids", [])
    63→            if not transition_task_ids:
    64→                return False, "chain_mode=True but no transition_task_ids (last chain join ID) provided"
    65→
    66→            # The single ID is the last join in the chain
    67→            last_join_id = transition_task_ids[0]
    68→            chain_output = db_ops.get_task_output_location_from_db(last_join_id)
    69→            if not chain_output:
    70→                return False, f"Failed to get output from chain join task {last_join_id}"
    71→
    72→            orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[FINAL_STITCH] Task {task_id}: Chain output from {last_join_id}: {chain_output[:100]}...")
    73→
    74→            # The chain join's output_location is the final video URL/path — use it directly.
    75→            # In chain mode, the last join_clips_child already muxes audio when is_last_join=True,
    76→            # so no additional audio processing is needed here.
    77→            audio_url = task_params_from_db.get("audio_url")
    78→            if audio_url:
    79→                orchestrator_logger.debug(f"[FINAL_STITCH] Task {task_id}: Audio requested \u2014 already muxed by last chain join, passthrough")
    80→

Perfect! I have all the information needed. Let me compile a comprehensive research report on the task flow:

## Task Flow Research Report: Submission to Generation

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's the complete task flow architecture:

### 1. TASK SUBMISSION

**Entry Points:**
- **Direct Queue:** Tasks submitted via `HeadlessTaskQueue.submit_task()` (task_queue.py:288)
- **Task Registry:** DB tasks routed through `TaskRegistry` (task_registry.py) which dispatches to specialized handlers
- **Queue Lifecycle:** `start_queue()` initializes worker threads and monitoring (queue_lifecycle.py:25)

**Task Submission Flow:**
```
submit_task() → task_queue.PriorityQueue.put() 
→ worker_loop() dequeues with get(timeout=1.0)
→ process_task_impl() begins execution
```

### 2. TASK STRUCTURE & CONVERSION

**GenerationTask Dataclass** (task_queue.py:63-78):
```python
@dataclass
class GenerationTask:
    id: str
    model: str  
    prompt: str
    parameters: Dict[str, Any]
    priority: int = 0
    status: str = "pending"  # pending, processing, completed, failed
```

**DB Task to Generation Task** (task_conversion.py:14):
- `db_task_to_generation_task()` converts DB rows to GenerationTask
- Applies parameter whitelist (80+ params including resolution, video_length, guidance_scale, etc.)
- Handles special task types (z_image, qwen, ltx2, travel, join)
- Parses phase_config if present

**Parameter Whitelist** includes:
- Basic: negative_prompt, resolution, video_length, num_inference_steps, guidance_scale, seed
- VACE: video_guide, video_mask, video_prompt_type, control_net_weight, control_net_weight2
- LoRA: activated_loras, loras_multipliers, additional_loras
- Advanced: flow_shift, guidance_phases, switch_threshold, sliding_window settings
- Image refs: image_start, image_end, image_refs, frames_positions
- Audio: audio_guide, audio_source, audio_prompt_type
- Qwen: style_reference_image, subject_reference_image, subject_strength
- Uni3C: use_uni3c, uni3c_guide_video, uni3c_strength, uni3c_keep_on_gpu

### 3. TASK PROCESSING PIPELINE

**Three-Phase Processing** (task_processor.py:45-241):

**Phase 1: Model Switching**
- `_switch_model()` loads the required model
- Uses `orchestrator.load_model()` checking WGP's ground truth (`wgp.transformer_type`)
- Tracks model switches in stats

**Phase 2: Billing Reset**
- `reset_generation_started_at()` resets billing timer after model load
- Ensures users not charged for model loading time

**Phase 3: Generation Execution**
- `_execute_generation()` delegates to orchestrator
- Applies phase config patches (if present)
- Acquires `_wgp_patch_lock` to prevent concurrent patch corruption
- Routes to appropriate generation method based on model type

### 4. ORCHESTRATOR GENERATION ROUTING

**Core Generate Method** (orchestrator.py:457-810):

Determines model type and routes:
- **VACE Models:** `generate_vace()` → calls `orchestrator.generate()` with video_guide required
- **Flux Models:** `generate_flux()` → maps video_length to num_images
- **T2V Models:** `generate_t2v()` → standard text-to-video path
- **Qwen Models:** `generate_with_config()` → typed config-based generation

**Critical VACE Requirement:**
- VACE models REQUIRE video_guide parameter (enforced at line 649-650)
- Raises ValueError if missing
- video_prompt_type determines VACE encoding (VP, VPD, VPDA, etc.)

**Parameter Resolution:**
- Resolves parameters via `_resolve_parameters()` (param_resolution.py)
- Applies model-specific defaults
- Sets default guidance_scale=7.5, batch_size=1, video_length=49

### 5. GENERATION MODES & INPUT REQUIREMENTS

**Task Types and Models** (task_types.py):

| Mode | Default Model | Required Inputs | Optional Inputs |
|------|---------------|-----------------|-----------------|
| t2v/t2v_22 | t2v/t2v_2_2 | prompt | resolution, video_length, guidance_scale |
| vace/vace_22 | vace_14B_cocktail_2_2 | prompt + video_guide | video_mask, video_prompt_type, control_net_weight |
| i2v/i2v_22 | i2v_14B | prompt + image_start | video_length |
| flux | flux | prompt | num_images (via video_length), guidance_scale |
| z_image_turbo | z_image | prompt | resolution, num_inference_steps |
| z_image_turbo_i2i | z_image_img2img | prompt + image_start | denoising_strength, resolution |
| ltx2/ltx2_multiframe | ltx2_19B | prompt | guide_images (for multiframe), image_start/image_end |
| qwen_image_edit | qwen_image_edit_20B | prompt + image | style/subject_reference (optional) |
| hunyuan | hunyuan | prompt | standard video params |

**Special Handling:**

*Qwen Image Tasks* (task_conversion.py:115-137):
- QwenHandler processes edit/style/inpaint variants
- Handles style_reference_image and subject_reference_image

*Z-Image I2I* (task_conversion.py:152-248):
- Downloads image from URL → local temp file
- Auto-scales to ~1MP preserving aspect ratio
- Rounds to 8-pixel multiples
- Passes as image_start parameter

*LTX2 Multiframe* (task_conversion.py:250-276):
- guide_images: list of {image, frame_idx, strength}
- Downloads URLs to local temp files
- Passed to generation

### 6. MULTI-SEGMENT ORCHESTRATION

**Travel Segments** (travel/orchestrator.py):

Handles "travel" tasks (smooth video transitions through key frames):
- Creates multiple travel_segment tasks
- Each segment generates a video
- Segments are stitched together with smooth transitions
- Supports SVI (Sliding Video Input) for frame continuation

**Key Parameters:**
- chain_segments: true = chain progressively, false = independent segments
- orchestrator_details: Contains segment definitions
- phase_config: Optional comprehensive phase config

**Travel Stitch Task** (travel/stitch.py):
- Stitches all segment outputs together
- Applies crossfade transitions between segments
- Muxes audio if provided

**Join Clips Tasks** (join/orchestrator.py, join/generation.py):

Pattern: Sequential chaining of clips
```
clip_A + clip_B → join_0 (no dependency)
↓
join_0 + clip_C → join_1 (depends on join_0)
↓
join_1 + clip_D → join_2 (depends on join_1, final output)
```

**Join Final Stitch** (join/final_stitch.py):
- Can operate in chain_mode (passthrough from last join)
- Or parallel mode (stitch clips + transitions in one pass)
- Muxes audio at final stage

### 7. ACTUAL GENERATION EXECUTION

**Generation Strategies** (generators/generation_strategies.py):

All three paths call `self.generate()` which:
1. Validates model/parameters
2. Resolves media paths (video_guide, video_mask, image_start, etc.)
3. Builds WGP parameter dictionary
4. Applies kwargs overrides
5. Calls WGP's `generate_video()` with captured stdout/stderr
6. Extracts output path from capture logs

**Key Generation Parameters Passed to WGP:**
- model_type: model identifier
- prompt: generation text
- num_inference_steps: denoising steps
- guidance_scale: CFG strength
- seed: reproducibility seed
- video_length: frame count
- resolution: output size (WxH format)
- batch_size: parallel frames
- All VACE/LTX2/Qwen specific parameters

**Post-Generation Processing:**
- Single-frame videos converted to PNG (if not travel segment)
- Output path extracted from WGP logs
- Result validated (file existence check with retry)

### 8. RESOLUTION & FRAME HANDLING

**Resolution Defaults:**
- Format: "WIDTHxHEIGHT" (e.g., "1280x720")
- Auto-snapped to model grid (varies by model)
- LTX2: 8-pixel multiples
- Z-Image: 8-pixel multiples

**Video Length Defaults:**
- T2V/VACE: 49 frames typical
- LTX2: Quantized in 8-frame steps, minimum 17 frames
- Flux: Mapped to num_images (4 typical)
- Single image: video_length=1

### 9. KEY FILES MAPPING

- **Queue System:** task_queue.py, task_processor.py, queue_lifecycle.py, download_ops.py
- **Task Conversion:** task_conversion.py, task_types.py, task_registry.py
- **Orchestration:** orchestrator.py, param_resolution.py, model_ops.py
- **Generation:** generators/generation_strategies.py, generators/wgp_params.py, generators/preflight.py
- **Multi-Segment:** travel/orchestrator.py, travel/segment_processor.py, join/orchestrator.py, join/final_stitch.py
- **Special Handlers:** Qwen (model_handlers/qwen_handler.py), LoRA (lora_setup.py), Uni3C (model_ops.py)

This architecture enables parallel multi-segment generation (travel/join tasks) while maintaining sequential queue processing, with centralized parameter resolution and model management via WanOrchestrator.

VIDEO_PROMPT_TYPE ENCODING PATTERNS\n\n### IC-LoRA Control Types (for video_guide):\n- `PVG` = Pose + Video Guide (structural control)\n- `DVG` = Depth + Video Guide (structural control)\n- `EVG` = Edge (canny) + Video Guide (structural control)\n- `PDVG` = Pose + Depth union + Video Guide (multi-control)\n- `PV` = Pose only (no guidance)\n- `G` = Guidance only (no structural control)\n- Generic: VP, VPD, VPDA (VACE types)\n\n---\n\n## 9. MULTI-FRAME GUIDE ARCHITECTURE\n\n### Frame Index Calculation:\n```python\ndef _to_latent_index(frame_idx, stride):\n    return int(frame_idx) // int(stride)\n```\n- stride typically = 8 for LTX-2\n- frame_idx=-1 resolves to frame_num-1\n- Maps frame positions to latent space positions\n\n### Guide Entry Format:\n```python\n{\n  \"image\": str,       # Path or PIL.Image\n  \"frame_idx\": int,   # 0 to N-1, or -1 for last\n  \"strength\": float,  # 0.0 to 1.0 influence\n}\n```\n\n### Processing Pipeline:\n1. task_conversion.py: Validates & resolves paths (HTTP → download)\n2. orchestrator.py: Converts paths to PIL.Image objects\n3. wgp_params.py: Passes as tuples (PIL, frame_idx, strength)\n4. ltx2.py: Processes via LTXVAddGuideMulti node\n\n---\n\n## 10. LORA HANDLING\n\n### Supported LoRA Types:\n1. **IC-LoRA** (Instruction Control)\n   - Union control: ltx-2-19b-ic-lora-union-control.safetensors\n   - Separate: pose, depth, canny variants\n   - Loaded via get_loras_transformer() in ltx2.py\n\n2. **Distilled LoRA**\n   - ltx-2-19b-distilled-lora-384.safetensors\n   - Used for fast inference (8 steps, CFG=1.0)\n   - Multiplier typically 0.7\n\n3. **Deforum Evolution LoRA**\n   - LTXV_2_Deforum-Evolution_v1.safetensors (3rd-party)\n   - Auto-downloaded from huggingface_hub\n   - Used for cinematic travel transitions\n\n### Parameter Format:\n```python\n\"lora_names\": [filename1, filename2, ...],\n\"lora_multipliers\": [1.0, 0.5, ...],\n# OR (older)\n\"activated_loras\": [filename1, ...],\n\"loras_multipliers\": \"1.0,0.5\",  # comma-separated string\n```\n\n---\n\n## 11. RESOLUTION & TIMING PATTERNS\n\n### Common Resolutions:\n- 768x512 (high quality, more VRAM)\n- 512x320 (medium, standard)\n- 384x256 (low VRAM testing)\n\n### Common Video Lengths:\n- 105 frames (full multiframe test)\n- 97 frames (pose workflow, 24fps ≈ 4s)\n- 33 frames (quick test, ~1.3s at 24fps)\n\n### Inference Steps:\n- 40 steps (high quality)\n- 20 steps (standard)\n- 8 steps (distilled/fast mode)\n\n### Guidance Scale (CFG):\n- 4.0 (standard)\n- 1.0 (distilled mode, no CFG)\n- 0.0 (unconditional)\n\n---\n\n## 12. # GPU Integration Test Suite Research

## 1. TASK TYPES & MODELS (from task_types.py)

### 27 Total Task Types across 2 categories:

#### WGP_TASK_TYPES (actual WGP generation):
- VACE models: vace, vace_21, vace_22

- Text-to-video: t2v, t2v_22, wan_2_2_t2i

- Qwen image: qwen_image_edit, qwen_image_style, image_inpaint, annotated_image_edit

@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def _chdir_to_wan2gp():
    # Must be in Wan2GP for WanOrchestrator

```python
import torch

    pytest.skip("No CUDA GPU available", allow_module_level=True)
```

### Orchestrator Creation:
```python
def _make_orchestrator(wan_root: str, output_dir: Path):
    os.environ.pop("HEADLESS_WAN2GP_SMOKE", None)  # Ensure REAL mode
    from headless_wgp import WanOrchestrator
    return WanOrchestrator(wan_root, main_output_dir=str(output_dir))

orch = _make_orchestrator(_chdir_to_wan2gp, output_dir)
switched = orch.load_model("ltx2_19B")
result = orch.generate(...)
assert os.path.isfile(result)
assert os.path.getsize(result) > 1000  # Sanity check
```

```python
assert result is not None, "generate() returned None"
assert os.path.isfile(result), f"Output not found: {result}"
size = os.path.getsize(result)
assert size > 1000, f"Output too small ({size} bytes), likely not a real video"
```

## 8. VIDEO_PROMPT_TYPE ENCODING PATTERNS

### IC-LoRA Control Types (for video_guide):
- `PVG` = Pose + Video Guide (structural control)
- `DVG` = Depth + Video Guide (structural control)
- `EVG` = Edge (canny) + Video Guide (structural control)
- `PDVG` = Pose + Depth union + Video Guide (multi-control)
- `PV` = Pose only (no guidance)
- `G` = Guidance only (no structural control)
- Generic: VP, VPD, VPDA (VACE types)

---

## 9. MULTI-FRAME GUIDE ARCHITECTURE

### Frame Index Calculation:
```python
def _to_latent_index(frame_idx, stride):
    return int(frame_idx) // int(stride)
```
- stride typically = 8 for LTX-2
- frame_idx=-1 resolves to frame_num-1
- Maps frame positions to latent space positions

### Guide Entry Format:
```python
{
  "image": str,       # Path or PIL.Image
  "frame_idx": int,   # 0 to N-1, or -1 for last
  "strength": float,  # 0.0 to 1.0 influence
}
```

### Processing Pipeline:
1. task_conversion.py: Validates & resolves paths (HTTP → download)
2. orchestrator.py: Converts paths to PIL.Image objects
3. wgp_params.py: Passes as tuples (PIL, frame_idx, strength)
4. ltx2.py: Processes via LTXVAddGuideMulti node

---

## 10. LORA HANDLING

### Supported LoRA Types:
1. **IC-LoRA** (Instruction Control)
   - Union control: ltx-2-19b-ic-lora-union-control.safetensors
   - Separate: pose, depth, canny variants
   - Loaded via get_loras_transformer() in ltx2.py

2. **Distilled LoRA**
   - ltx-2-19b-distilled-lora-384.safetensors
   - Used for fast inference (8 steps, CFG=1.0)
   - Multiplier typically 0.7

3. **Deforum Evolution LoRA**
   - LTXV_2_Deforum-Evolution_v1.safetensors (3rd-party)
   - Auto-downloaded from huggingface_hub
   - Used for cinematic travel transitions

### Parameter Format:
```python
"lora_names": [filename1, filename2, ...],
"lora_multipliers": [1.0, 0.5, ...],
# OR (older)
"activated_loras": [filename1, ...],
"loras_multipliers": "1.0,0.5",  # comma-separated string
```

---

## 11. RESOLUTION & TIMING PATTERNS

### Common Resolutions:
- 768x512 (high quality, more VRAM)
- 512x320 (medium, standard)
- 384x256 (low VRAM testing)

### Common Video Lengths:
- 105 frames (full multiframe test)
- 97 frames (pose workflow, 24fps ≈ 4s)
- 33 frames (quick test, ~1.3s at 24fps)

### Inference Steps:
- 40 steps (high quality)
- 20 steps (standard)
- 8 steps (distilled/fast mode)

### Guidance Scale (CFG):
- 4.0 (standard)
- 1.0 (distilled mode, no CFG)
- 0.0 (unconditional)

### **7. IC-LoRA Control Encoding (video_prompt_type)**

- **`PVG`** = Pose + Video Guide
- **`DVG`** = Depth + Video Guide
- **`EVG`** = Edge (canny) + Video Guide
- **`PDVG`** = Pose + Depth union + Video Guide
- **`PV`** = Pose only (no guidance)
- **`VP`, `VPD`, `VPDA`** = VACE control types

### **8. Multi-Frame Guide Architecture**

**Guide entry format:**
```python
{
  "image": str,          # File path or PIL.Image
  "frame_idx": int,      # 0 to N-1, or -1 for last frame
  "strength": float,     # 0.0–1.0 influence weight
}
```

**Processing pipeline:**
1. `task_conversion.py`: Resolves paths, downloads HTTP URLs
2. `orchestrator.py`: Converts paths to PIL.Image
3. `wgp_params.py`: Passes as tuples (PIL, frame_idx, strength)
4. `ltx2.py`: Processes via LTXVAddGuideMulti node with latent injection

**Frame index to latent space mapping:**
```python
def _to_latent_index(frame_idx, stride):
    return int(frame_idx) // int(stride)  # stride=8 for LTX-2
```

### **11. Smoke vs. Real GPU Tests**

**Smoke tests** (no GPU needed):
- Set `HEADLESS_WAN2GP_SMOKE=1`
- Create tiny 64x64 PIL images
- Create 128-byte placeholder videos
- Test parameter flow only
- Found in: `test_ltx2_pose_smoke.py`, `test_ltx2_multiframe_smoke.py`

**Real GPU tests** (require CUDA + model weights):
- Unset `HEADLESS_WAN2GP_SMOKE`
- Generate actual videos (>1000 bytes)
- Assert output file exists and has meaningful size
- Auto-skip if `torch.cuda.is_available() == False`

## Key Observations for Test Suite Design

1. **Asset management**: Tests reference media files (Donald1_00003.mp4, 1.jpg–5.jpg) that aren't committed. Your existing tests handle this by either:
   - Creating synthetic images on-the-fly (PIL)
   - Auto-downloading LoRAs via HuggingFace
   - Expecting files to be manually placed

2. **LTX-2 is the primary model tested**: 4 of 5 GPU tests use LTX-2 19B, covering:
   - Base multi-frame guides
   - IC-LoRA depth/pose/canny control
   - IC-LoRA + multi-frame combined
   - Travel between images with LoRA

3. **Video_prompt_type encodes control type**: Single-letter combinations (P, V, D, E, G) control which IC-LoRA variant and features are active

4. **Multi-frame guides use latent-space injection**: Frame indices divided by stride (8) to map to latent positions; frame_idx=-1 resolves to last frame

5. **Common test duration is ~33 frames (1.3s)**: Balances thoroughness with quick execution
